### PART 2 — CATEGORY B GOODS[^key-88bc9a84c7e1aa78ac6b1fb12cb218b2]

#### 9. Small arms and light weapons within ML1 and ML2

Goods specified in entry ML1.a, ML1.b, ML1.c or ML2.a in Schedule 2 that are designed to be carried, operated and fired by an individual or by three or fewer individuals acting together, other than mortars with a calibre of 100mm or more.

#### 10. Accessories and ammunition for small arms and light weapons within ML1 and ML2

The following goods—

- (a) accessories specified in entry ML1.d or ML2.c. in Schedule 2 that are capable of being used in connection with weapons falling within paragraph 9;
- (b) weapon sights specified in entry ML5.a. in Schedule 2 that are designed for use within weapons falling within paragraph 9; and
- (c) ammunition that is capable of being fired or launched by weapons falling within paragraph 9.

#### 10A. Non-military Firearms[^key-f065b57fa4fc89b76a657acae3cfc819]

Firearms, their parts and essential components and ammunition specified in entry PL9010 or PL9011 in Schedule 3.[^key-ffca8df041b2c528eb2670d337dc4f2d]

#### 11. Light weapons within ML4

Equipment specified in entry ML4.b. in Schedule 2 that is—

- (a) specially designed for firing or launching rockets, grenades, missiles or other explosive devices; and
- (b) designed to be carried, operated and fired by an individual or by three or fewer individuals acting together.

#### 12. Ammunition for light weapons within ML4

Rockets, grenades, missiles and other explosive devices that are—

- (a) specified in entry ML4 in Schedule 2; and
- (b) capable of being fired or launched from equipment falling within paragraph 11.

#### 13. Hand grenades

Grenades specified in entry ML4 in Schedule 2 that are designed to be thrown.

#### 14. MANPADS, missiles for them, associated equipment and their specially designed components

To the extent they do not fall within paragraph 11 or 12, the following goods—

- (a) man-portable air defence systems (MANPADS), as follows:
  - (i) surface-to-air missile systems designed to be man-portable and operated and fired by a single individual;
  - (ii) surface-to-air missile systems designed to be operated and fired by more than one individual acting as a crew and portable by several individuals;
- (b) missiles for MANPADS;
- (c) “production” equipment specially designed for MANPADS;
- (d) field test equipment specially designed for MANPADS;
- (e) specialised training equipment and simulators for MANPADS.

#### 15. Long-range missiles

Missiles capable of a range of 300km or more which fall within Schedule 2.

#### 16. Anti-vehicle landmines

Land mines designed to be placed under, on or near the ground or other surface area and to be exploded by the presence, proximity or contact of a vehicle.

#### 17. Components for “goods” within this Part

Components specially designed for goods falling within any of paragraphs 9 to 16.

#### 18. Battle tanks and armoured combat vehicles within ML6

Vehicles specified in entry ML6.a in Schedule 2 as follows—

- (a) Tracked or wheeled self-propelled armoured fighting vehicles with an unladen weight of 16.5 metric tonnes or more and with a main gun with a calibre of 75mm or more;
- (b) Tracked, semi-tracked or wheeled self-propelled vehicles, with armoured protection, as follows:
  - (i) designed and equipped to transport a squad of four or more infantrymen; or
  - (ii) armed with an integral weapon with a calibre of 12.5mm or more or a missile launcher.

#### 19. Large-calibre artillery systems within ML2 and ML4

To the extent that they do not fall within paragraph 9 or 11, the following goods—

- (a) Guns or howitzers specified in entry ML2.a of Schedule 2 with a calibre of 75mm or more;
- (b) Mortars specified in entry ML2.a of Schedule 2 with a calibre of 100mm or more; and
- (c) Multiple-launch rocket systems specified in entry ML4.b of Schedule 2 with a calibre of 75 mm or more.

#### 20. Combat aircraft and attack helicopters within ML10

“Combat aircraft” and “attack helicopters” specified in entry ML10.a or ML10.c of Schedule 2.

#### 21. Warships within ML9

Vessels and submarines specified in entry ML9.a of Schedule 2 as follows—

- (a) Having a standard displacement of 500 metric tons or above; or
- (b) Having a standard displacement of less than 500 metric tons and equipped for launching missiles or torpedoes with a range of 25km or more.

#### 22. Other missiles and missile launchers

To the extent they are not covered elsewhere in this Part, the following goods specified in entry ML4 of Schedule 2—

- (a) rockets or missiles capable of a range of 25km or more other than ‘ground-to-air missiles’;
- (b) Equipment designed or modified for launching missiles or rockets in sub-paragraph (a).

*Note:* *Paragraph 22 includes remotely piloted vehicles with the characteristics for missiles as defined above.*

*Technical Note:*

*‘ground-to-air missiles’ means those surface-to-air missiles which are mounted on fixed land sites or on wheeled or tracked mobile launchers.*

## SCHEDULE 2 — MILITARY GOODS, SOFTWARE AND TECHNOLOGY[^key-fdfdb1d11ff036598f1fa218d7eb8593]

Article 2

*Note: In this Schedule, defined terms are printed in quotation marks.*

Definitions

In this Schedule:

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td colspan="6">. . .</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“airship” means a power-driven airborne vehicle that is kept buoyant by a body of gas (usually helium, formerly hydrogen) which is lighter than air;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“biocatalyst” means enzymes for specific chemical or biochemical reactions or other biological compounds which bind to and accelerate the degradation of chemical warfare (CW) agents;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“biological agents” means pathogens or toxins, selected or modified (such as altering purity, shelf life, virulence, dissemination characteristics, or resistance to UV radiation) to produce casualties in humans or animals, degrade equipment or damage crops or the environment;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">. . .</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“cyber incident response” means the process of exchanging necessary information or “software” on a cybersecurity incident with individuals or organisations responsible for conducting or coordinating remediation to address the cybersecurity incident;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">‘Deactivation Regulation’ means <a href="https://www.legislation.gov.uk/eur/2015/2403">Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) 2015/2403</a> establishing common guidelines on deactivation standards and techniques for ensuring that deactivated firearms are rendered irreversibly inoperable;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“development” means all stages prior to “production” (e.g. design, design research, design analyses, design concepts, assembly and testing of prototypes, pilot production schemes, design data, process of transforming design data into goods or “software”, configuration design, integration design, layouts);</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“digital computer” means equipment which can, in the form of one or more discrete variables, perform all of the following:</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="4"></td><td>a.</td><td>accept data,</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="4"></td><td>b.</td><td>store data or instructions in fixed or alterable (writable) storage,</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="4"></td><td>c.</td><td>process data by means of a stored sequence of instructions which is modifiable, and</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="4"></td><td>d.</td><td>provide output of data;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="4"></td><td colspan="2"><em>Technical Note:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="4"></td><td colspan="2"><em>Modifications of a stored sequence of instructions include replacement of fixed storage devices, but not a physical change in wiring or interconnections;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“end-effectors” means grippers, active tooling units (i.e. devices for applying motive power, process energy or sensing to the workpiece) and any other tooling that is attached to the baseplate on the end of a “robot” manipulator arm</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“energetic materials” means substances or mixtures that react chemically to release energy required for their intended application; “explosives”, “pyrotechnics” and “propellants” are sub-classes of energetic materials;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“equivalent standards” means comparable national or international standards recognised by one or more Wassenaar Arrangement “participating states” and applicable to the relevant entry;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“explosives” means solid, liquid or gaseous substances or mixtures of substances which, in their application as primary, booster, or main charges in warheads, demolition and other applications, are required to detonate;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">. . .</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“first generation image intensifier tubes” means electrostatically focused tubes, employing input and output fibre optic or glass face plates, multi-alkali photocathodes (S-20 or S-25), but not microchannel plate amplifiers;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“fuel cell” means an electrochemical device that converts chemical energy directly into Direct Current (DC) electricity by consuming fuel from an external source;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“improvised explosive devices” means devices fabricated or intended to be placed in an improvised manner incorporating destructive, lethal, noxious, “pyrotechnic” or incendiary chemicals designed to destroy, disfigure or harass; they may incorporate military stores, but are normally devised from non-military components;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“laser” means an item that produces spatially and temporally coherent light through amplification by stimulated emission of radiation;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“library” (parametric technical database) means a collection of technical information, reference to which may enhance the performance of relevant systems, equipment or components;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“lighter-than-air vehicles” means balloons and “airships” that rely on hot air or on lighter-than-air gases such as helium or hydrogen for their lift;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“nuclear reactor” means the goods within or attached directly to the reactor vessel, the equipment which controls the level of power in the core, and the components which normally contain, come into direct contact with or control the primary coolant of the reactor core;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“participating state” means a state participating in the Wassenaar Arrangement (see www.wassenaar.org);</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“production” means all production stages (e.g. product engineering, manufacture, integration, assembly (mounting), inspection, testing, quality assurance);</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“propellants” means substances or mixtures that react chemically to produce large volumes of hot gases at controlled rates to perform mechanical work;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“ pyrotechnics ” means mixtures of solid or liquid fuels and oxidisers which, when ignited, undergo an energetic chemical reaction at a controlled rate intended to produce specific time delays, or quantities of heat, noise, smoke, visible light or infrared radiation; pyrophorics are a subclass of “pyrotechnics”, which contain no oxidisers but ignite spontaneously on contact with air;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“required” as applied to “technology”, refers to only that portion of “technology” which is peculiarly responsible for achieving or exceeding the controlled performance levels, characteristics or functions. Such “required” “technology” may be shared by different goods ...;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“riot control agents” means substances which under the expected conditions of use for riot control purposes, produce rapidly in humans sensory irritation or disabling physical effects which disappear within a short time following termination of exposure;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6"><em>Technical Note:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6"><em>Tear gases are a subset of “riot control agents”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“robot” means a manipulation mechanism, which may be of the continuous path or of the point-to-point variety, may use sensors, and which:</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2">a.</td><td colspan="2">is multifunctional;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2">b.</td><td colspan="2">is capable of positioning or orienting material, parts, tools or special devices through variable movements in three dimensional space;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2">c.</td><td colspan="2">incorporates three or more closed or open loop servo-devices which may include stepping motors; and</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2">d.</td><td colspan="2">has “user-accessible programmability” by means of the teach/playback method or by means of an electronic computer which may be a programmable logic controller, i.e. without mechanical intervention;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6"><em>Note:</em><br><em>This definition does not include:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>a.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>manipulation mechanisms which are only manually/teleoperator controllable;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>b.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>fixed sequence manipulation mechanisms, which are automated moving devices, operating according to “programmes” where the motions are limited by fixed stops, such as pins or cams and the sequence of motions and the selection of paths or angles are not variable or changeable by mechanical, electronic or electrical means;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>c.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>mechanically controlled variable sequence manipulation mechanisms, which are automated moving devices, operating according to “programmes” where the motions are limited by fixed, but adjustable stops, such as pins or cams and the sequence of motions and the selection of paths or angles are variable within the fixed programme pattern; variations or modifications of the programme pattern (such as changes of pins or exchanges of cams) in one or more motion axes are accomplished only through mechanical operations;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>d.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>non-servo-controlled variable sequence manipulation mechanisms, which are automated moving devices, operating according to mechanically fixed programmed motions; the “programme” is variable but the sequence proceeds only by the binary signal from mechanically fixed electrical binary devices or adjustable stops;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>e.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>stacker cranes defined as Cartesian coordinate manipulator systems manufactured as an integral part of a vertical array of storage bins and designed to access the contents of those bins for storage or retrieval.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">. . .</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“satellite navigation system” means a system consisting of ground stations, a constellation of satellites, and receivers, that enables receiver locations to be calculated on the basis of signals received from the satellites. It includes global navigation satellite systems and regional navigation satellite systems;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“<strong>Spacecraft</strong>” means a craft designed to operate in, persist in or transit through space in the form of a “satellite”, “space probe”, or “space vehicle”.</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="5"><em>Note:</em></td><td><em>“<strong>Spacecraft</strong>” does not include landers, rovers, or other craft, limited by design to operate on or beneath the surface of, or in the atmosphere of an extra-terrestrial celestial body, or “sub-orbital craft”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“<strong>sub-orbital craft</strong>” means a craft having an enclosure designed for the transport of people or cargo which is designed to:<br>a. operate above the stratosphere;<br>b. perform a non-orbital trajectory; and<br>c. land back on Earth with the people or cargo intact;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">. . .</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“superconductive” in relation to materials (e.g. metals, alloys or compounds) means those which can lose all electrical resistance (i.e. which can attain infinite electrical conductivity and carry very large electrical currents without Joule heating); the superconductive state of a material is individually characterised by a ‘critical temperature’, a critical magnetic field, which is a function of temperature, and a critical current density which is a function of both magnetic field and temperature;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6"><em>Technical Note:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6"><em>‘Critical temperature’ (also known as the transition temperature) of a specific “superconductive” material means the temperature at which the specific material loses all resistance to the flow of direct electrical current.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“technology” means specific ‘information’ necessary for the “development”; “production” or “use” of goods or “software”;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6"><em>Technical Note:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6"><em>‘Information’ may take forms including, not limited to: blueprints, plans, diagrams, models, formulae, algorithms, tables, ‘source code’, engineering designs and specifications, manuals and instructions written or recorded on other media or devices (e.g. disk, tape, read-only memories);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6"><em>‘source code’ (or source language) is a convenient expression of one or more processes which may be turned by a programming system into equipment executable form.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“Unmanned Aerial Vehicle” (“UAV”) means any “aircraft” capable of initiating flight and sustaining controlled flight and navigation without any human presence on board;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“use” means operation, installation (e.g. on-site installation), maintenance, checking, repair, overhaul and refurbishing;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“user-accessible programmability” means the facility allowing a user to insert, modify or replace “programmes” by means other than:</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="4"></td><td>a.</td><td>A physical change in writing or interconnections; or</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="4"></td><td>b.</td><td>The setting of function controls including entry of parameters;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">“vulnerability disclosure” means the process of identifying, reporting, or communicating a vulnerability to, or analysing a vulnerability with, individuals or organisations responsible for conducting or coordinating remediation for the purpose of resolving the vulnerability.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-dd391a1766d149741ea355089ac9d083][^key-a009cd528ffd9ecd0352d44a8d2e60c9][^key-72ae350c3169accd32ff97ecb5363cc1][^key-a0a288dcd2f9150253dc49af339f4d73][^key-2ef8bafa7fcbbfda028907f5a343b0b9][^key-ac4ee5194b2d840cd099683a3b7ff856][^key-adcb99653431696c573936c4e965ed5d][^key-07b5689cca0b4fc092d30b6a17b00494][^key-4be66461597d312a6cd30036b111fb89][^key-8dbd628ca0c49f6f0936574f3559606f][^key-b0e27ebfc889badc826802ed7e8783bd][^key-218aedb15429b4bf0b48db9c6f875059][^key-a0094384198fd81e10f786baabc70d62][^key-0bcdf93cb18eceb8445184a5cab72c01][^key-05c3933fccc22e13d4e479e2651a2517][^key-34e655e0c13a41af8109caaa63aa99b5][^key-392a9aa6681c8d7786e8705ba364a7c9][^key-e422140e04325d408d335772cc0e95e7][^key-8381e4332f905784a6a0b6b919fc6756][^key-4311ae4961a0d1c0c00f4e8bdf9b11a7][^key-5023fedb044767d982323018ced20250]

### Military, Security and Para-military Goods, Software and Technology and Arms, Ammunition and Related Material

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML1</strong></td><td colspan="7">Smooth-bore weapons with a calibre of less than 20 mm, other firearms and automatic weapons with a calibre of 12.7 mm (calibre 0.50 inches) or less and accessories, as follows, and specially designed components therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td colspan="6"><em>Weapons using non-centre fire (e.g. rimfired) cased ammunition and which are not of the fully automatic firing type are specified in PL9010.a. or PL9011.a. of</em> Schedule 3.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td colspan="6"><em>ML1 does not control:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>a.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Firearms specially designed for dummy ammunition and which are incapable of discharging a projectile;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>b.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Firearms specially designed to launch tethered projectiles, having no high explosive charge or communications link, to a range of 500 m or less;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>c.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Firearms to which the “Deactivation Regulation” applies which have been deactivated and marked in accordance with the technical specifications set out in Annexes I and II to that Regulation;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>d.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Firearms to which the “Deactivation Regulation” does not apply which bear a mark and are certified as having been rendered incapable of discharging any shot, bullet or other missile in accordance with section 8 of the Firearms (Amendment) Act 1988</em><em>.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>e.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Weapons using non-centre fire (e.g. rimfired) cased ammunition and which are not of the fully automatic firing type.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="6">Rifles and combination guns, handguns, machine, sub-machine and volley guns;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="6"><em>N.B.: Rifles and combination guns, manufactured earlier than 1938 are specified in PL9010.a. and PL9011.a. of</em> Schedule 3.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="6"><em>Note: ML1.a. does not control:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"></td><td><em>a.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Bayonets;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"></td><td><em>b.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Rifles and combination guns manufactured earlier than 1938;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"></td><td><em>c.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Reproductions of rifles and combination guns, the originals of which were manufactured earlier than 1890;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"></td><td><em>d.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Handguns, volley guns and machine guns, manufactured earlier than 1890, and their reproductions;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"></td><td><em>e.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Rifles or handguns, specially designed to discharge an inert projectile by compressed air or CO<sub>2</sub></em> ;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"></td><td><em>f.</em></td><td colspan="2">Handguns specially designed for any of the following:<br>1. slaughtering of domestic animals; or<br>2. tranquillising of animals.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="6">Smooth-bore weapons as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>N.B.1.:</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Smooth-bore weapons manufactured earlier than 1938 are specified in PL9010.a. and PL9011.a. of Schedule 3.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>N.B.2.:</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Smooth-bore weapons manufactured in 1938 or later are specified in PL9010.a. and PL9011.a. of Schedule 3 provided they are not specifically designed for military use or of the fully automatic type.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3">1.</td><td colspan="3">Smooth-bore weapons specially designed for military use;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3">2.</td><td colspan="3">Fully automatic smooth-bore weapons not controlled by ML1.b.1.;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>Note:</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>ML1.b. does not control:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2"><em>a.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Smooth-bore weapons manufactured earlier than 1938;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2"><em>b.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Reproductions of smooth-bore weapons, the originals of which were manufactured earlier than 1890;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2"><em>c.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Smooth-bore weapons, specially designed for any of the following;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2"></td><td><em>1.</em></td><td><em>Slaughtering of domestic animals;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2"></td><td><em>2.</em></td><td><em>Tranquillising of animals;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2"></td><td><em>3.</em></td><td><em>Seismic testing;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2"></td><td><em>4.</em></td><td><em>Firing of industrial projectiles;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2"><em>d.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Signal pistols;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2"><em>e.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Industrial Tools;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2"><em>f.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Smooth-bore weapons that are both not specially designed for military use and specially designed to discharge an inert projectile by compressed air or CO<sub>2</sub></em>.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="6">Weapons using caseless ammunition;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td rowspan="9">d.</td><td colspan="6">Accessories designed for firearms specified in ML1.a., ML1.b. or ML1.c., as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td rowspan="8"></td><td colspan="6">1. Detachable cartridge magazines;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">2. Sound suppressors or moderators;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">3. ‘Gun-mountings’;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6"><em>Technical Note:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6"><em>For the purposes of ML1.d.3., a ‘gun-mounting’ is a fixture designed to mount a gun onto a ground “vehicle”, “aircraft”, “vessel” or structure.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">4. Flash suppressors;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">5. Optical weapon-sights with electronic image processing;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6">6. Optical weapon-sights specially designed for military use.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-725ef39392d60f36eae7b53a4b7e969e][^key-274497119baad2b2c8123ccd2fa09d44][^key-550a04178cc1cbd85fb733c4a4ee186d][^key-d9dc7ee1d442e389fff61615a04a2c3f][^key-9d7e098a3a8bcf2f76b13e7885a82048][^key-d7180d48380bfed5e1ba1e83309f5e9d][^key-a19321adb222c9d3546c1bc7db2bdead][^key-8c787803b83f03c2c0ecb3ad93c88516][^key-af207739a1d9726eb246d6fd60ba22a5][^key-1b3eeb43e30f69cede0f0ea00685f59d][^key-6609d51551a720c010e071bf82b3fb0d]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML2</strong></td><td colspan="5">Smooth-bore weapons with a calibre of 20 mm or more, other armament or weapons with a calibre greater than 12.7 mm (calibre 0.50 inches), accessories and projectors specially designed or modified for military use, as follows, and specially designed components therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="5">. . .</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="4">Guns, howitzers, cannon, mortars, anti-tank weapons, projectile launchers, military flame throwers, rifles, recoilless rifles and smooth-bore weapons;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4"><em>N.B.1.: Rifles, smooth-bore weapons and combination guns, manufactured earlier than 1938 are specified in PL9010.a. and PL9011.a. of Schedule 3.</em><br><em>N.B.2.: Smooth-bore weapons that are manufactured in 1938 or later are specified in PL9010.a. and PL9011.a. of Schedule 3, provided they are not specially designed for military use or not of the fully automatic firing type.</em><br>Note: <em>ML2.a. does not control:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>a.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Rifles, smooth-bore weapons and combination guns, manufactured earlier than 1938;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>b.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Reproductions of rifles, smooth-bore weapons and combination guns, the originals of which were manufactured earlier than 1890;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>c.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Guns, howitzers, cannons, mortars, manufactured earlier than 1890;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>d.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Smooth-bore weapons specially designed for any of the following:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>1.</em></td><td><em>Slaughtering of domestic animals;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>2.</em></td><td><em>Tranquillising of animals;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>3.</em></td><td><em>Seismic testing;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>4.</em></td><td><em>Firing of industrial projectiles;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>e.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Signal pistols;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>f.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Hand-held projectile launchers, specially designed to launch tethered projectiles, having no high explosive charge or communications link, to a range of 500 m or less.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>g.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Smooth-bore weapons that are not of the following:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>1.</em></td><td><em>Specially designed for military use; or</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>2.</em></td><td><em>Fully Automatic firing type</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="4">The following types of projectors, when specially designed or modified for military use:<br>1. Smoke canister projectors;<br>2. Gas canister projectors;<br>3. Pyrotechnics projectors.<br><em>Note: ML2.b. does not control signal pistols.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="4">Accessories specially designed for the weapons specified in ML2.a., as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="3">Weapon sights and weapon sight mounts, specially designed for military use;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="3">Signature reduction devices;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>3</td><td colspan="3">Mountings;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>4</td><td colspan="3">Detachable cartridge magazines.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>d.</td><td colspan="4">. . .</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-35dd7a45b5064d28cac3b50bf93eadd7][^key-cacb0b0916d404eb08659a95a6aa143d][^key-7920149a3844208550f84b4caca3839e][^key-a652367ca1c1ecf9b7c77cf522c40bab][^key-e87a549cc929761ccb368cfc9ba559af][^key-4848fc719bd050fb083467d81f18f44f][^key-fd063c4c9987a159b394edbdd24bdfcd][^key-d1cd4a16d266cefdf01b116859f39827][^key-0cb7bcdfff5f38ff5a9ffad8de2cbe93][^key-1eecb8928179b73134bdf1dbf2ae19a1]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML3</strong></td><td colspan="4">Ammunition and fuze setting devices, as follows, and specially designed components therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="4"><em>N.B.: Ammunition specially designed for “firearms” specified in PL9010.a. or PL9011.a. in</em> Schedule 3 <em>is specified in PL9010.c. or PL9011.c. in</em> Schedule 3<em>.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="3">Ammunition for weapons specified in ML1, ML2 or ML12;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>Note: ML3.a. does not control:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>a.</em></td><td><em>Ammunition crimped without a projectile (blank star);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>b.</em></td><td><em>Dummy ammunition with a pierced powder chamber;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>c.</em></td><td><em>Other blank and dummy ammunition, not incorporating components designed for live ammunition;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>d.</em></td><td><em>Components specially designed for blank or dummy ammunition, specified in this Note a, b or c; or</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>e.</em></td><td><em>Cartridges specially designed for signalling, bird scaring or lighting of gas flares at oil wells.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="3">Fuze setting devices specially designed for ammunition specified in ML3.a.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-076d2e1a6a58f716481ad7d7e442cbaf]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML4</strong></td><td colspan="3">Bombs, torpedoes, rockets, missiles, other explosive devices and charges, and related equipment and accessories, as follows, and specially designed components therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>N.B. 1: Electronic guidance and navigation equipment is controlled in ML11.a.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>N.B. 2: Aircraft missile protection systems are controlled in ML4.c.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="2">Bombs, torpedoes, grenades, smoke canisters, rockets, mines, missiles, depth charges, demolition-charges, demolition-devices, demolition-kits, “pyrotechnic” devices, cartridges, submunitions therefor and simulators (i.e., equipment simulating the characteristics of any of these items), specially designed for military use;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"><em>N.B.: For grenade or canister ammunition for weapons or projectors specified in ML1. or ML2. and submunitions specially designed for ammunition, see ML3.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="2">Equipment that is both specially designed for military use and specially designed for ‘activities’ relating to any of the following:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td>goods specified in ML4.a.; or</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td>“improvised explosive devices”;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Technical Note:</em></td><td><em>For the purposes of ML4.b., ‘activities’ applies to handling, controlling, activating, powering with one-time operational output, launching, laying, sweeping, discharging, decoying, jamming, detonating, disrupting, detecting or disposing.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td><em>ML4.b. does not control hand-held devices limited by design solely to the detection of metal objects and incapable of distinguishing between mines and other metal objects.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="2">Aircraft missile protection systems (AMPS) not specified in ML4.b.1..</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-f27caedbecab0c789d2a31deecb3cf80][^key-4439f590cf9756325582289e5c771814][^key-d9ef20d27480b8565de0eb6f16b078f4]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML5</strong></td><td colspan="2">Fire control, surveillance and warning equipment, and related systems, test and alignment and countermeasure equipment, as follows, specially designed for military use, and specially designed components and accessories therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td>Weapon sights, bombing computers, gun laying equipment and weapon control systems;</td></tr>
<tr><td rowspan="4"></td><td rowspan="4">b.</td><td>Other fire control, surveillance and warning equipment, and related systems, as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td>1.Target acquisition, designation, range-finding, surveillance or tracking systems;</td></tr>
<tr><td>2. Detection, recognition or identification equipment;</td></tr>
<tr><td>3. Data fusion or sensor integration equipment;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>c.</td><td>Countermeasure equipment for goods specified in ML5.a. or ML5.b.;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note: For the purposes of ML5.c. countermeasure equipment includes detection equipment.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>d.</td><td>Field test or alignment equipment, specially designed for goods specified in ML5.a., ML5.b. or ML5.c.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-6eeee8c43e369fd693ef9999968a8d05][^key-0ea7f321acb450bdde8e178c1685953e]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML6</strong></td><td colspan="5">Ground “vehicles” and components as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="5"><em>N.B.: Electronic guidance and navigation equipment is controlled in ML11.a.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="4">Ground “vehicles” and components therefor, specially designed or modified for military use;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">. . .</td></tr>
<tr><td rowspan="2"></td><td rowspan="2"></td><td colspan="3"><em>Note 1:</em></td><td><em>ML6.a. includes trailers.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="3"><em>Note 2:</em></td><td><em>In ML6.a. modification of a ground “vehicle” for military use entails a structural, electrical or mechanical change involving one or more specially designed military components.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td rowspan="9"></td><td rowspan="9">b.</td><td colspan="4">Other ground “vehicles” and components, as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td>1.</td><td colspan="3">“Vehicles” having all of the following:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="2">manufactured or fitted with materials or components to provide ballistic protection equal to or better than level III (NIJ 0108.01, September 1985) or “equivalent standards”;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="2">a transmission to provide drive to both front and rear wheels simultaneously, including those vehicles having additional wheels for load bearing purposes whether driven or not;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="2">‘Gross Vehicle Weight Rating (GVWR)’ greater than 4,500 kg; and</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>d.</td><td colspan="2">designed or modified for off-road use;</td></tr>
<tr><td>2.</td><td colspan="3">Components meeting both of the following descriptions:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="2">specially designed for “vehicles” specified in ML6.b.1.; and</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="2">providing ballistic protection equal to or better than level III (NIJ 0108.01, September 1985) or “equivalent standards”;</td></tr>
<tr><td rowspan="2"></td><td rowspan="2"></td><td colspan="2" rowspan="2"><em>Technical Notes:</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>1. NIJ 0108.01 means the National Institute of Justice standard for Ballistic Resistance for Protective Materials.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2"><em>2. ‘Gross Vehicle Weight Rating (GVWR)’ is also known as Maximum Authorised Mass, Gross Vehicle Weight or Permissible Maximum Weight.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4"><em>N.B.: See also ML13.a. for armoured plate.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4"><em>Note 1: ML6.b. does not control “vehicles” designed or modified for transporting money or valuables .</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4"><em>Note 2: ML6.b. does not control “vehicles” fitted with, or designed or modified to be fitted with, a plough, flail or tiller for the purpose of land mine clearance.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4"><em>Note 3: ML6 does not control “vehicles” that meet all of the following descriptions:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>a.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>were manufactured before 1946;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>b.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>do not have items specified in this Schedule and manufactured after 1945, except for reproductions of original components or accessories for the vehicle; and</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>c.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>do not incorporate weapons specified in ML1, ML2 or ML4 unless they are inoperable and incapable of discharging a projectile, including:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>1.</em></td><td><em>in the case of firearms to which the “Deactivation Regulation” applies, by having been deactivated and marked in accordance with the technical specifications set out in Annexes I and II to that Regulation;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>2.</em></td><td><em>in the case of firearms to which the “Deactivation Regulation” does not apply, by bearing a mark and being certified as having been rendered incapable of discharging any shot, bullet or other missile in accordance with section 8 of the Firearms (Amendment) Act 1988.</em></td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-1a14005e989bc32913375ba0dda582e2][^key-cd2417fc7231ee8c2fb687edcb149488][^key-71437353b8d1dd4df0429717ce91c048][^key-33e3b757af5a3e391776be5b5130930f][^key-33b31f002ecd380d3933e1398278857e]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML7</strong></td><td colspan="6">Chemical agents, “biological agents”, toxic chemicals and mixtures containing such agents or chemicals, “riot control agents”, radioactive materials, related equipment, components and materials, as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"><em>Note:</em></td><td colspan="4"><em>In some instances chemicals are listed by name and Chemical Abstracts Service (CAS) number. Chemicals of the same structural formula (e.g. hydrates, isotopically-labelled forms or all possible stereoisomers) are controlled regardless of name or CAS number. CAS numbers are shown to assist in identifying whether a particular chemical or mixture is controlled, irrespective of nomenclature. CAS numbers cannot be used as unique identifiers because some forms of the listed chemical have different CAS numbers, and mixtures containing a listed chemical may also have different CAS numbers.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="5">“Biological agents” or radioactive materials selected or modified to increase their effectiveness in producing casualties in humans or animals, degrading equipment or damaging crops or the environment;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="5">Chemical warfare (CW) agents including, but not limited to, the following:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="4">CW nerve agents:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="3">O-Alkyl (equal to or less than C<sub>10</sub>, including cycloalkyl) alkyl (Methyl, Ethyl, n-Propyl or Isopropyl)-phosphonofluoridates, such as:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3">Sarin (GB):O-Isopropyl methylphosphonofluoridate (CAS 107-44-8); and</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3">Soman (GD):O-Pinacolyl methylphosphonofluoridate (CAS 96-64-0);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="3">O-Alkyl (equal to or less than C<sub>10</sub>, including cycloalkyl) N,N-dialkyl (Methyl, Ethyl, n-Propyl or Isopropyl) phosphoramidocyanidates, such as:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3">Tabun (GA):O-Ethyl N,N-dimethylphosphoramidocyanidate (CAS 77-81-6);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="3">O-Alkyl (H or equal to or less than C<sub>10</sub>, including cycloalkyl) S-2-dialkyl (Methyl, Ethyl, n-Propyl or Isopropyl)-aminoethyl alkyl (Methyl, Ethyl, n-Propyl or Isopropyl) phosphonothiolates and corresponding alkylated and protonated salts, such as:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3">VX: O-Ethyl S-2-diisopropylaminoethyl methyl phosphonothiolate (CAS 50782-69-9);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="4">CW vesicant agents:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="3">Sulphur mustards, such as:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="2">2-Chloroethylchloromethylsulphide (CAS 2625-76-5);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="2">Bis(2-chloroethyl) sulphide (CAS 505-60-2);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="2">Bis(2-chloroethylthio) methane (CAS 63869-13-6);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>4.</td><td colspan="2">1,2-bis (2-chloroethylthio) ethane (CAS 3563-36-8);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>5.</td><td colspan="2">1,3-bis (2-chloroethylthio)-n-propane (CAS 63905-10-2);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>6.</td><td colspan="2">1,4-bis (2-chloroethylthio)-n-butane (CAS 142868-93-7);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>7.</td><td colspan="2">1,5-bis (2-chloroethylthio)-n-pentane (CAS 142868-94-8);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>8.</td><td colspan="2">Bis (2-chloroethylthiomethyl) ether (CAS 63918-90-1);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>9.</td><td colspan="2">Bis (2-chloroethylthioethyl) ether (CAS 63918-89-8);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="3">Lewisites, such as:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="2">2-chlorovinyldichloroarsine (CAS 541-25-3);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="2">Tris (2-chlorovinyl) arsine (CAS 40334-70-1);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="2">Bis (2-chlorovinyl) chloroarsine (CAS 40334-69-8);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="3">Nitrogen mustards, such as:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="2">HN1: bis (2-chloroethyl) ethylamine (CAS 538-07-8);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="2">HN2: bis (2-chloroethyl) methylamine (CAS 51-75-2);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="2">HN3: tris (2-chloroethyl) amine (CAS 555-77-1);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="4">CW incapacitating agents, such as:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="3">3-Quinuclidinyl benzilate (BZ) (CAS 6581-06-2);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>4.</td><td colspan="4">CW defoliants, such as:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="3">Butyl 2-chloro-4-fluorophenoxyacetate (LNF);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="3">2,4,5-trichlorophenoxyacetic acid (CAS 93-76-5) mixed with 2,4-dichlorophenoxyacetic acid (CAS 94-75-7) (Agent Orange (CAS 39277-47-9));</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="5">CW binary precursors and key precursors, as follows, and chemical mixtures containing one or more of these precursors:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="4">Alkyl (Methyl, Ethyl, n-Propyl or Isopropyl) Phosphonyl Difluorides, such as:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">DF: Methyl Phosphonyldifluoride (CAS 676-99-3);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="4">O-Alkyl (H equal to or less than C<sub>10</sub>, including cycloalkyl) O-2-dialkyl (Methyl, Ethyl, n-Propyl or Isopropyl)-aminoethyl alkyl (Methyl, Ethyl, n-Propyl or Isopropyl) phosphonites and corresponding alkylated and protonated salts, such as:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">QL: O-Ethyl O-2-di-isopropylaminoethyl methylphosphonite (CAS 57856-11-8);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="4">Chlorosarin: O-Isopropyl methylphosphonochloridate (CAS 1445-76-7);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>4.</td><td colspan="4">Chlorosoman: O-Pinacolyl methylphosphonochloridate (CAS 7040-57-5);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>d.</td><td colspan="5">“Riot control agents”, active constituent chemicals and combinations thereof including:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="4">α-Bromobenzeneacetonitrile, (Bromobenzyl cyanide) (CA) (CAS 5798-79-8);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="4">[(2-chlorophenyl) methylene] propanedinitrile, (o-Chlorobenzylidenemalononitrile) (CS) (CAS 2698-41-1);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="4">2-Chloro-1-phenylethanone, Phenylacyl chloride (ω-chloroacetophenone) (CN) (CAS 532-27-4);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>4.</td><td colspan="4">Dibenz-(b,f)-1,4-oxazephine (CR) (CAS 257-07-8);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>5.</td><td colspan="4">10-Chloro-5,10-dihydrophenarsazine, (Phenarsazine chloride), (Adamsite), (DM) (CAS 578-94-9);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>6.</td><td colspan="4">N-Nonanoylmorpholine, (MPA) (CAS 5299-64-9);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note 1:</em></td><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>ML7.d. does not control “riot control agents” individually packaged for personal self-defence purposes.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note 2:</em></td><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>ML7.d. does not control active constituent chemicals and combinations thereof identified and packaged for food production or medical purposes.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>e.</td><td colspan="5">Equipment specially designed or modified for military use, designed or modified for the dissemination of any of the following, and specially designed components therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="4">Materials or agents specified in ML7.a., ML7.b. or ML7.d.;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="4">CW agents made up of precursors specified in ML7.c.;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>f.</td><td colspan="5">Protective and decontamination goods, specially designed or modified for military use, components and chemical mixtures as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="4">Goods designed or modified for defence against materials specified in ML7.a., ML7.b. or ML7.d. and specially designed components therefor;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>See also 1A of Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="4">Goods designed or modified for decontamination of goods contaminated with materials specified in ML7.a. or ML7.b. and specially designed components therefor;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="4">Chemical mixtures specially developed or formulated for the decontamination of goods contaminated with materials specified in ML7.a. or ML7.b.;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>g.</td><td colspan="5">Goods specially designed or modified for military use, designed or modified for the detection or identification of materials specified in ML7.a., ML7.b. or ML7.d. and specially designed components therefor;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>See also 1A of Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>ML7.g. does not control personal radiation monitoring dosimeters.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>h.</td><td colspan="5">‘Biopolymers’ specially designed or processed for the detection or identification of CW agents specified in ML7.b., and the cultures of specific cells used to produce them;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">Technical Notes: For the purposes of ML7.h.:</td><td>(1) <em>‘Biopolymers’ are biological macromolecules as follows:</em><br>(a) <em>Enzymes for specific chemical or biochemical reactions;</em><br>(b) <em>‘Anti-idiotypic’, ‘monoclonal’ or ‘polyclonal’ ‘antibodies’;</em><br>(c) <em>Specially designed or specially processed ‘receptors’.</em><br>(2) <em>‘Anti-idiotypic antibodies’ means antibodies which bind to the specific antigen binding sites of other antibodies.</em><br>(3) <em>‘Monoclonal antibodies’ means proteins which bind to one antigenic site and are produced by a single clone of cells.</em><br>(4) <em>‘Polyclonal antibodies’ means a mixture of proteins which bind to the specific antigen and are produced by more than one clone of cells.</em><br>(5) <em>‘Receptors’ means biological macromolecular structures capable of binding ligands, the binding of which affects physiological functions.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>i.</td><td colspan="5">“Biocatalysts” for the decontamination or degradation of CW agents, and biological systems therefor, as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="4">“Biocatalysts” specially designed for the decontamination or degradation of CW agents specified in ML7.b., and resulting from directed laboratory selection or genetic manipulation of biological systems;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="4">Biological systems containing the genetic information specific to the “production” of “biocatalysts” specified in ML7.i.1. as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="3">‘Expression vectors’;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>Technical Note:</td><td colspan="3">For the purposes of ML7.i.2.a., ‘expression vectors’ are carriers (e.g., plasmid or virus) used to introduce genetic material into host cells.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="3">Viruses;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="3">Cultures of cells.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note 1:</em></td><td colspan="4"><em>ML7.b. and ML7.d. do not control:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>a.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Cyanogen chloride (CAS 506-77-4);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"><em>N.B.:</em></td><td><em>See 1C of Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>b.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Hydrocyanic acid (CAS 74-90-8);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>c.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Chlorine (CAS 7782-50-5);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>d.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Carbonyl chloride (phosgene) (CAS 75-44-5);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"><em>N.B.:</em></td><td><em>See 1C of Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>e.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Diphosgene (trichloromethyl-1-chloroformate) (CAS 503-38-8);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>f.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>This entry is not used;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>g.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Xylyl bromide: ortho: (CAS 89-92-9), meta: (CAS 620-13-3), para: (CAS 104-81-4);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>h.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Benzyl bromide (CAS 100-39-01);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>i.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Benzyl iodide (CAS 620-05-3);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>j.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Bromo acetone (CAS 598-31-2);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>k.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Cyanogen bromide (CAS 506-68-3);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>l.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Bromo methylethylketone (CAS 816-40-0);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>m.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Chloro acetone (CAS 78-95-5);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>n.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Ethyl iodoacetate (CAS 623-48-3);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>o.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Iodo acetone (CAS 3019-04-3);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>p.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Chloropicrin (CAS 76-06-2);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"><em>N.B.:</em></td><td><em>See 1C of Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>q.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Pelargonic acid vanillylamide (PAVA) (CAS 2444-46-4);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"><em>N.B.:</em></td><td><em>See 3.2. of Annex III to “the torture Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>r.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Oleoresin capsicum (OC) (CAS 8023-77-6).</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"><em>N.B.:</em></td><td><em>See 3.3. of Annex III to “the torture Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note 2:</em></td><td colspan="4"><em>The cultures of cells and biological systems specified in ML7.h. and ML7.i.2. are exclusive and ML7.h. and ML7.i.2. do not include cells or biological systems for civil purposes, (e.g. agricultural, pharmaceutical, medical, veterinary, environmental, waste management, or in the food industry).</em></td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-0b26ba95535c6f58377a65cfd55cb24c][^key-ddc28602733d59929b1ef19cf1af41ab][^key-06f12f4520b3bebe358939a238c13045][^key-3447a5f8547a7f89d819c034ba5ecd46][^key-a4a30f3fa74dac604efbf894a8ef9aee][^key-e503b68b87b06a560c4063a0205be8e0][^key-2eeb53bb28a1a7eb83621c8ada098f7a][^key-0a62a8b5face12b21c5ba29677f8aaa1]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML8</strong></td><td colspan="7">“Energetic materials”, and related substances, as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"><em>N.B.:</em></td><td colspan="5"><em>Charges and devices are controlled in ML4 and 1A008 of Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td></td><td colspan="5"><em>In some instances chemicals are listed by name and Chemical Abstracts Service (CAS) number. Chemicals of the same structural formula (e.g. hydrates, isotopically-labelled forms or all possible stereoisomers) are controlled regardless of name or CAS number. CAS numbers are shown to assist in identifying whether a particular chemical or ‘mixture’ is controlled, irrespective of nomenclature. CAS numbers cannot be used as unique identifiers because some forms of the listed chemical have different CAS numbers, and ‘mixtures’ containing a listed chemical may also have different CAS numbers.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"><em>Technical Note:</em></td><td><em>1.</em></td><td colspan="4"><em>For the purposes of ML8, excluding ML8.c.11. and ML8.c.12., a ‘mixture’ refers to a composition of two or more substances with at least one substance being controlled in ML8.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td><em>2.</em></td><td colspan="4"><em>For the purposes of ML8, particle size is the mean particle diameter on a weight or volume basis. International or equivalent national standards will be used in sampling and determining particle size.</em></td></tr>

<tr><td></td><td></td><td>34.</td><td colspan="5">This entry is not used;</td></tr>

<tr><td></td><td></td><td>39.</td><td colspan="5">Energetic ionic materials melting between 343 K (70°C) and 373 K (100°C) and with detonation velocity exceeding 6,800 m/s or detonation pressure exceeding 18 GPa (180 kbar);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>40.</td><td colspan="5">BTNEN (Bis(2,2,2-trinitroethyl)-nitramine) (CAS 19836-28-3);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>41.</td><td colspan="5">FTDO (5,6-(3’,4’-furazano)-1,2,3,4-tetrazine-1,3-dioxide);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>42.</td><td colspan="5">EDNA (Ethylenedinitramine) (CAS 505-71-5);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>43.</td><td colspan="5">TKX-50 (Dihydroxylammonium 5,5’-bistetrazole-1,1’-diolate).</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td colspan="5"><em>ML8.a. includes ‘explosive co-crystals’.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Technical Note:</em></td><td colspan="5"><em>For the purposes of ML8.a., an ‘explosive co-crystal’ is a solid material consisting of an ordered three dimensional arrangement of two or more explosive molecules, where at least one is specified in ML8.a.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="6">“Propellants” as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="5">Any solid “propellant” with a theoretical specific impulse (under standard conditions) of more than:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="4">240 seconds for non-metallised, non-halogenised “propellant”;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="4">250 seconds for non-metallised, halogenised “propellant”;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="4">260 seconds for metallised “propellant”;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="5">This entry is not used;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="5">“Propellants” having a force constant of more than 1,200 kJ/kg;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>4.</td><td colspan="5">“Propellants” that can sustain a steady-state linear burning rate of more than 38 mm/s under standard conditions (as measured in the form of an inhibited single strand) of 6.89 MPa (68.9 bar) pressure and 294 K (21<sup>o</sup>C);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>5.</td><td colspan="5">Elastomer Modified Cast Double Base (EMCDB) “propellants” with extensibility at maximum stress of more than 5% at 233 K (-40<sup>o</sup>C);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>6.</td><td colspan="5">Any “propellant” containing substances specified in ML8.a.;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>7.</td><td colspan="5">“Propellants” not specified elsewhere in this Schedule, specially designed for military use.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="6">“Pyrotechnics”, fuels and related substances, as follows, and ‘mixtures’ thereof:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="5">“Aircraft” fuels specially formulated for military purposes;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="5"><em>Note 1: ML8.c.1. does not control the following “aircraft” fuels: JP-4, JP-5, and JP-8.</em><br><em>Note 2: “Aircraft” fuels controlled in ML8.c.1. are finished goods, not their constituents.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="5">Alane (aluminium hydride) (CAS 7784-21-6);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="5">Boranes, as follows, and their derivatives:<br>a. Carboranes;<br>b. Borane homologues, as follows:<br>1. Decaborane (14) (CAS 17702-41-9);<br>2. Pentaborane (9) (CAS 19624-22-7);<br>3. Pentaborane (11) (CAS 18433-84-6);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>4.</td><td colspan="5">Hydrazine and derivatives as follows (see also ML8.d.8. and ML8.d.9. for oxidising hydrazine derivatives);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="4">Hydrazine (CAS 302-01-2) in concentrations of 70% or more;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"><em>Note:</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>ML8.c.4.a. does not control hydrazine ‘mixtures’ specially formulated for corrosion control.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="4">Monomethyl hydrazine (CAS 60-34-4);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="4">Symmetrical dimethyl hydrazine (CAS 540-73-8);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>d.</td><td colspan="4">Unsymmetrical dimethyl hydrazine (CAS 57-14-7);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>5.</td><td colspan="5">Metal fuels, fuel ‘mixtures’ or “pyrotechnic” mixtures, in particle form whether spherical, atomised, spheroidal, flaked or ground, manufactured from material consisting of 99% or more of any of the following:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="4">Metals as follows and ‘mixtures’ thereof:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3">1.</td><td>Beryllium (CAS 7440-41-7) in particle sizes of less than 60µm;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3">2.</td><td>Iron powder (CAS 7439-89-6) with particle size of 3µm or less produced by reduction of iron oxide with hydrogen;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="4">‘Mixtures’ containing any of the following:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3">1.</td><td>Zirconium (CAS 7440-67-7), magnesium (CAS 7439-95-4) or alloys of these in particle sizes of less than 60µm;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3">2.</td><td>Boron (CAS 7440-42-8) or boron carbide (CAS 12069-32-8) fuels of 85% purity or higher and particle sizes of less than 60µm;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note 1:</em></td><td colspan="4"><em>ML8.c.5.b.2 does not control boron and boron carbide enriched with boron-10 (20% or more of total boron-10 content).</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note 2:</em></td><td colspan="4"><em>ML8.c.5.b. only controls metal fuels in particle form when they are mixed with other substances to form a ‘mixture’ formulated for military purposes such as liquid “propellant” slurries, solid “propellants” or “pyrotechnic” mixtures.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note 3:</em></td><td colspan="4"><em>ML8.c.5. controls “explosives” and fuels, whether or not the metals or alloys are encapsulated in aluminium, magnesium, zirconium or beryllium.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td colspan="4"><em>See also 1C of Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>6.</td><td colspan="5">Military material containing thickeners for hydrocarbon fuels specially formulated for use in flame throwers or incendiary munitions, such as metal stearates (e.g., octal (CAS 637-12-7) or palmitates;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>7.</td><td colspan="5">Perchlorates, chlorates and chromates composited with powdered metal or other high energy fuel components;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>8.</td><td colspan="5">Spherical or spheroidal aluminium powder (CAS 7429-90-5) with a particle size of 60μm or less, and manufactured from material with an aluminium content of 99% or more;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>9.</td><td colspan="5">Titanium subhydride (TiH<sub>n</sub>) of stoichiometry equivalent to n = 0.65-1.68;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>10.</td><td colspan="5">Liquid high energy density fuels not specified in ML8.c.1., as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="4">Mixed fuels, that incorporate both solid and liquid fuels (e.g., boron slurry), having a mass-based energy density of 40 MJ/kg or greater;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="4">Other high energy density fuels and fuel additives (e.g., cubane, ionic solutions, JP-7, JP-10), having a volume-based energy density of 37.5 GJ per cubic meter or greater, measured at 293 K (20°C) and one atmosphere (101.325 kPa) pressure;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">Note: <em>ML8.c.10.b. does not control ... fossil refined fuels or biofuels, or fuels for engines certified for use in civil aviation.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>11.</td><td colspan="5">“Pyrotechnic” and pyrophoric materials as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="4">“Pyrotechnic” or pyrophoric materials specifically formulated to enhance or control the production of radiated energy in any part of the IR spectrum;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="4">Mixtures of magnesium, polytetrafluoroethylene (PTFE) and a vinylidene difluoride- hexafluoropropylene copolymer (e.g., MTV);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>12.</td><td colspan="5">Fuel mixtures, “pyrotechnic” mixtures or “energetic materials”, not specified elsewhere in ML8, having all of the following:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="4">Containing greater than 0.5% of particles of any of the following:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">1.</td><td colspan="2">Aluminium;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">2.</td><td colspan="2">Beryllium;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">3.</td><td colspan="2">Boron;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">4.</td><td colspan="2">Zirconium;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">5.</td><td colspan="2">Magnesium; or</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">6.</td><td colspan="2">Titanium;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="4">Particles specified in ML8.c.12.a. with a size less than 200 nm in any direction; and</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="4">Particles specified in ML8.c.12.a. with a metal content of 60% or greater;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="6"><em>Note:</em><em>ML8.c.12. includes thermites.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>d.</td><td colspan="6">Oxidisers, as follows, and ‘mixtures’ thereof:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="5">ADN (ammonium dinitramide or SR 12) (CAS 140456-78-6);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="5">AP (ammonium perchlorate) (CAS 7790-98-9);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="5">Compounds composed of fluorine and any of the following:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="4">Other halogens;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="4">Oxygen; or</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="4">Nitrogen;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note 1:</em></td><td colspan="4"><em>ML8.d.3. does not control chlorine trifluoride (CAS 7790-91-2).</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note 2:</em></td><td colspan="4"><em>ML8.d.3. does not control nitrogen trifluoride in its gaseous state (CAS 7783-54-2).</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>Note 3:</td><td colspan="4">ML8.d.3. does not control iodine pentafluoride (CAS 7783-66-6).</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td colspan="4"><em>See also 1C of Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>4.</td><td colspan="5">DNAD (1,3-dinitro-1,3-diazetidine) (CAS 78246-06-7);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>5.</td><td colspan="5">HAN (hydroxylammonium nitrate) (CAS 13465-08-2);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>6.</td><td colspan="5">HAP (hydroxylammonium perchlorate) (CAS 15588-62-2);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>7.</td><td colspan="5">HNF (hydrazinium nitroformate) (CAS 20773-28-8);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>8.</td><td colspan="5">Hydrazine nitrate (CAS 37836-27-4);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>9.</td><td colspan="5">Hydrazine perchlorate (CAS 27978-54-7);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>10.</td><td colspan="5">Liquid oxidisers comprised of or containing inhibited red fuming nitric acid (IRFNA) (CAS 8007-58-7);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>e.</td><td colspan="6">Binders, plasticisers, monomers and polymers as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="5">AMMO (azidomethylmethyloxetane and its polymers) (CAS 90683-29-7);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="5">BAMO (3,3-bis(azidomethyl)oxetane and its polymers) (CAS 17607-20-4);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="5">BDNPA (bis (2,2-dinitropropyl)acetal) (CAS 5108-69-0);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>4.</td><td colspan="5">BDNPF (bis (2,2-dinitropropyl)formal) (CAS 5917-61-3);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>5.</td><td colspan="5">BTTN (butanetrioltrinitrate) (CAS 6659-60-5);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>6.</td><td colspan="5">Energetic monomers, plasticisers or polymers specially formulated for military use and containing any of the following:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="4">Nitro groups;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="4">Azido groups;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="4">Nitrate groups;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>d.</td><td colspan="4">Nitraza groups; or</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>e.</td><td colspan="4">Difluoroamino groups;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>7.</td><td colspan="5">FAMAO (3-difluoroaminomethyl-3-azidomethyl oxetane) and its polymers;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>8.</td><td colspan="5">FEFO (bis-(2-fluoro-2,2-dinitroethyl) formal) (CAS 17003-79-1);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>9.</td><td colspan="5">FPF-1 (poly-2,2,3,3,4,4-hexafluoropentane-1,5-diol formal) (CAS 376-90-9);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>10.</td><td colspan="5">FPF-3 (poly-2,4,4,5,5,6,6-heptafluoro-2-tri-fluoromethyl-3-oxaheptane-1,7-diol formal);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>11.</td><td colspan="5">GAP (glycidylazide polymer) (CAS 143178-24-9) and its derivatives;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>12.</td><td colspan="5">HTPB (hydroxyl terminated polybutadiene) with a hydroxyl functionality equal to or greater than 2.2 and less than or equal to 2.4, a hydroxyl value of less than 0.77 meq/g, and a viscosity at 30<sup>o</sup>C of less than 47 poise (CAS 69102-90-5);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>13.</td><td colspan="5">Alcohol functionalised poly(epichlorohydrin) with a molecular weight of less than 10,000, as follows:</td></tr>

<tr><td></td><td></td><td>18.</td><td colspan="5">TVOPA (1,2,3-tris[1,2-bis(difluoroamino)ethoxy] propane or tris vinoxy propane adduct) (CAS 53159-39-0);</td></tr>

<tr><td></td><td></td><td>21.</td><td colspan="5">TMETN (Trimethylolethane trinitrate) (CAS 3032-55-1);</td></tr>

<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="5">Basic copper salicylate (CAS 62320-94-9);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="5">BHEGA (bis-(2-hydroxyethyl) glycolamide) (CAS 17409-41-5);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="5">BNO (butadienenitrileoxide);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>4.</td><td colspan="5">Ferrocene derivatives as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="4">Butacene (CAS 125856-62-4);</td></tr>

<tr><td></td><td></td><td>11.</td><td colspan="5">MAPO (tris-1-(2-methyl)aziridinyl phosphine oxide) (CAS 57-39-6), and BOBBA 8 (bis(2-methyl aziridinyl)-2-(2-hydroxypropanoxy) propylamino phosphine oxide); and other MAPO derivatives;</td></tr>

<tr><td></td><td></td><td>14.</td><td colspan="5">3-Nitraza-1,5-pentane diisocyanate (CAS 7406-61-9);</td></tr>

<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="4">Titanium IV, [(2-propenolato-1) methyl, n-propanolatomethyl] butanolato-1, tris(dioctyl)phosphate;</td></tr>

<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="4">1,1R,1S-trimeoyl-tris(2-ethylaziridine) (HX-868, BITA) (CAS 7722-73-8);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="4">Polyfunctional aziridine amides with isophthalic, trimesic, isocyanuric or trimethyladipic backbone also having a 2-methyl or 2-ethyl aziridine group;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td colspan="4"><em>ML8.f.17.b. includes:</em></td></tr>

<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>b.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>2,4,6-tris(2-ethyl-1-aziridinyl)-1,3,5-triazine (HX-874) (CAS 18924-91-9);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>c.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>1,1’-trimethyladipoyl-bis(2-ethylaziridine)(HX-877) (CAS 71463-62-2).</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>18.</td><td colspan="5">Propyleneimine (2-methylaziridine) (CAS 75-55-8);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>19.</td><td colspan="5">Superfine iron oxide (Fe<sub>2</sub>O<sub>3</sub>)(CAS 1317-60-8) with a specific surface area more than 250 m<sup>2</sup>/g and an average particle size of 3.0 nm or less;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>20.</td><td colspan="5">TEPAN (tetraethylenepentaamineacrylonitrile) (CAS 68412-45-3); cyanoethylated polyamines and their salts;</td></tr>
