<tr><td></td><td></td><td>22.</td><td colspan="5">TPB (triphenyl bismuth) (CAS 603-33-8);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>23.</td><td colspan="5">TEPB (Tris (ethoxyphenyl) bismuth) (CAS 90591-48-3);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>g.</td><td colspan="6">Precursors as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="5">BCMO (3,3-bis(chloromethyl)oxetane) (CAS 78-71-7);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="5">Dinitroazetidine-t-butyl salt (CAS 125735-38-8);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="5">Hexaazaisowurtzitane derivates including HBIW (hexabenzylhexaazaisowurtzitane) (CAS 124782-15-6) (see also ML8.a.4.) and TAIW (tetraacetyldibenzylhexaazaisowurtzitane) (CAS 182763-60-6) (see also ML8.a.4.);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>4.</td><td colspan="5">This entry is not used;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>5.</td><td colspan="5">TAT (1,3,5,7-tetraacetyl-1,3,5,7-tetraaza cyclo-octane) (CAS 41378-98-7);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>6.</td><td colspan="5">1,4,5,8-tetraazadecalin (CAS 5409-42-7);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>7.</td><td colspan="5">1,3,5-trichlorobenzene (CAS 108-70-3);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>8.</td><td colspan="5">1,2,4-trihydroxybutane (1,2,4-butanetriol) (CAS 3068-00-6);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>9.</td><td colspan="5">DADN (1,5-diacetyl-3,7-dinitro-1,3,5,7-tetraaza-cyclooctane) (see also ML8.a.13.);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>h.</td><td colspan="6">‘Reactive material’ powders and shapes, as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="5">Powders of any of the following materials, with a particle size less than 250 µm in any direction and not specified elsewhere in ML8:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">a.</td><td>Aluminium;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">b.</td><td>Niobium;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">c.</td><td>Boron;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">d.</td><td>Zirconium;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">e.</td><td>Magnesium;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">f.</td><td>Titanium;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">g.</td><td>Tantalum;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">h.</td><td>Tungsten;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">i.</td><td>Molybdenum; or</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">j.</td><td>Hafnium;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="5">Shapes, not specified in ML3, ML4, ML12 or ML16, fabricated from powders specified in ML8.h.1.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="6"><em>Technical Notes:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="6">For the purposes of ML8.h.:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>1.</em></td><td colspan="5"><em>‘Reactive materials’ are designed to produce an exothermic reaction only at high shear rates and for use as liners or casings in warheads.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>2.</em></td><td colspan="5"><em>‘Reactive material’ powders are produced by, for example, a high energy ball milling process.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>3.</em></td><td colspan="5"><em>‘Reactive material’ shapes are produced by, for example, selective laser sintering.</em></td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-446df4e2ca7e66ff8003c78cb5c6d0d4][^key-0015a37350142c080ce86d4ccfadc82a][^key-6569e593a23b2d827b9a6afac9d70adf][^key-f08453f26695889caced6e62ba763bd7][^key-57eb1464c00008f3527185b847534944][^key-d130dbe0d9e7e40d76c3ae5e7b0e272d][^key-5b9e80f8577489f754a0c03ae67c6bc6][^key-e260671d1307b3b23509ddbbceac57bd][^key-b549c03e8253df3fecffc7b1642af931][^key-f8eee7377411f3eb70e18f97ac724395][^key-4451379ec7be2b57e79b6bd92ab2aaa9][^key-66df293191d120044b9fd3a3fc0b58b0][^key-14c3dbf71fcc07e9418288416651e849][^key-77c4d0350c41bdb30d950aea18788831][^key-e61e95a747c69650715f582286933a6f][^key-07e4e122bc2417da4409b3eec0d0e921][^key-b3450ae5fbbb0d2da2dc9a036d26b39d][^key-c9e79f169cb6bb7a41e367afa6d57d3b][^key-88ad713e931040fcb5cd3fcb2993373f][^key-ef61abb044513bb75d03ca95fe1a4081][^key-59c449dab5834a35ed7afee2b4ee92f6][^key-d85235bc9023b69dadd8988624dd770a][^key-95e61522b5529ec521c5861edd850611][^key-52940c78faebbcb9aa9cd8cc7d85caa8]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML9</strong></td><td colspan="12">“Vessels” of war, special naval equipment, accessories, components and other surface “vessels”, as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td colspan="11"><em>Electronic guidance and navigation equipment is controlled in ML11.a.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td rowspan="4"></td><td colspan="2" rowspan="4">a.</td><td colspan="10">“Vessels” and components as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">1.</td><td colspan="3">“Vessels” (surface or underwater) specially designed or modified for military use, regardless of current state of repair or operating condition, and whether or not they contain weapon delivery systems or armour, and components therefor specially designed or modified for military use;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7"><em>Note:</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>ML9.a.1. includes vehicles specially designed or modified for delivery of divers.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">2.</td><td colspan="3">Surface “vessels” not specified in ML9.a.1., having any of the following, fixed or integrated into the “vessel”:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td></td><td colspan="6">a.</td><td colspan="3">Automatic weapons having a calibre of 12.7 mm or greater specified in ML1, or weapons specified in ML2, ML4, ML12 or ML19, or ‘mountings’ or hard points for weapons having a calibre of 12.7mm or greater;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="9"><em>Technical Note:</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>For the purposes of ML9.a.2.a., ‘mountings refers to weapon mounts or structural strengthening for the purposes of installing weapons.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td></td><td colspan="6">b.</td><td colspan="3">Fire control systems specified in ML5;</td></tr>
<tr><td rowspan="3"></td><td colspan="2" rowspan="3"></td><td></td><td colspan="6">c.</td><td colspan="3">Both:</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="3">1.</td><td colspan="3">‘CBRN protection’; and</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="4">2.</td><td colspan="3">‘Pre-wet or wash down system’ designed for decontamination purposes; or</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="9"><em>Technical Notes:</em></td><td colspan="2"></td><td><em>For the purposes of ML9.a.2.c.2., 'pre-wet or wash down system' is a seawater spray system capable of simultaneously wetting the exterior superstructure and decks of a vessel.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td rowspan="5"></td><td colspan="2" rowspan="5"></td><td></td><td colspan="4">d.</td><td colspan="3"></td><td colspan="2">Active weapon countermeasure systems specified in ML4.b., ML5.c. or ML11.a. but only where the “vessel” has any of the following:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="5"></td><td colspan="2">1.</td><td colspan="2">‘CBRN protection’;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="5"></td><td colspan="2">2.</td><td colspan="2">Hull and superstructure, specially designed to reduce the radar cross section;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="5"></td><td colspan="2">3</td><td colspan="2">Thermal signature reduction devices, (e.g. an exhaust gas cooling system), excluding those specially designed to increase overall power plant efficiency or to reduce the environmental impact; or</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="5"></td><td colspan="2">4</td><td colspan="2">A degaussing system designed to reduce the magnetic signature of the whole vessel.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"><em>Technical Note:</em></td><td></td><td colspan="5"></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2">For the purposes of ML9.a.2., 'CBRN protection' is a self-contained interior space containing features such as over-pressurisation, isolation of ventilation systems, limited ventilation openings with CBRN filters and limited personnel access points incorporating air-locks.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2">b.</td><td colspan="10">Anti-submarine nets and anti-torpedo nets, specially designed for military use;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2">c.</td><td colspan="10">Hull penetrators and connectors specially designed for military use, that enable interaction with equipment external to a “vessel”, and components therefor specially designed for military use.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-ae59dd038b92b4f5c79e05c5dc0dc7bc][^key-faa0aafebc14abdbc95c17f189178f33][^key-32f7d474a0a53096d4734b7512d8d074][^key-3fda6cb0c8852955dd48cd2493fad287][^key-f90214d5c588741944db8159b246bef3][^key-a91a3a2a4be47af3bb109f396e676faa]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML10</strong></td><td colspan="5">“Aircraft”, “lighter-than-air vehicles”, “Unmanned Aerial Vehicles” (“UAVs”), aero-engines , “sub-orbital craft” and “aircraft” equipment, related goods and components, as follows, specially designed or modified for military use:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="5"><em>N.B.:Electronic guidance and navigation equipment is controlled in ML11.a.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="4">Manned “aircraft” and “lighter-than-air vehicles”, and specially designed components therefor;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td colspan="4"><em>ML10.a. does not control “aircraft” or “lighter-than-air vehicles” meeting all of the following descriptions:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4"><em>a.</em><em>were first manufactured before 1946;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4"><em>b.</em><em>do not incorporate items specified in this Schedule unless the items are required to meet safety or airworthiness standards; and</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4"><em>c.</em><em>do not incorporate weapons specified in this Schedule, unless they have been rendered inoperable and incapable of being returned to operation, including:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4"><em>1.</em><em>in the case of firearms to which the “Deactivation Regulation” applies, by having been deactivated and marked in accordance with the technical specifications set out in Annexes I and II to that Regulation;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4"><em>2.</em><em>in the case of firearms to which the “Deactivation Regulation” does not apply, by bearing a mark and being certified as having been rendered incapable of discharging any shot, bullet or other missile in accordance with section 8 of the Firearms (Amendment) Act 1988.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="4">This entry is not used.</td></tr>
<tr><td rowspan="4"></td><td rowspan="4">c.</td><td colspan="4">Unmanned “aircraft” and related equipment, as follows, and specially designed components therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td>1.</td><td colspan="3">“UAVs” Remotely Piloted Air Vehicles (RPVs), autonomous programmable vehicles and unmanned “lighter-than-air vehicles”;</td></tr>
<tr><td>2.</td><td colspan="3">Launchers, recovery equipment and ground support equipment;</td></tr>
<tr><td>3.</td><td colspan="3">Equipment designed for command and control;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>d.</td><td colspan="4">Propulsion aero-engines and specially designed components therefor;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td colspan="4"><em>ML10.d. does not control propulsion aero-engines that were first manufactured before 1946.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>e.</td><td colspan="4">Airborne refuelling equipment specially designed or modified for any of the following, and specially designed components therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="3">“Aircraft” specified in ML10.a.; or</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="3">Unmanned “aircraft” specified in ML10.c;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>f.</td><td colspan="4">Ground equipment specially designed for “aircraft” specified in ML10.a. or aero-engines specified in ML10.d.;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note 1:</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>ML10.f. includes pressure refuelling equipment and equipment designed to facilitate operations in confined areas, including equipment located on board a ship.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note 2:</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>ML10.f. does not control:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>1.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Towbars;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>2.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Protective mats and covers;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>3.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Ladders, steps and platforms;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>4.</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>Chocks, lashings and tie-down equipment.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>g.</td><td colspan="4">Aircrew life support equipment, aircrew safety equipment and other devices for emergency escape, not specified in ML10.a., designed for “aircraft” specified in ML10.a. or “sub-orbital craft” specified in ML10.j.;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>ML10.g. does not control aircrew helmets that do not incorporate, or have mountings or fittings for, equipment specified in this Schedule.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>For helmets, see also ML13.c.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>h.</td><td colspan="4">Parachutes, paragliders and related equipment, as follows, and specially designed components therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="3">Parachutes, other than those which form part of items controlled elsewhere in this Schedule;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="3">Paragliders;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="3">Equipment specially designed for high altitude parachutists;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>i.</td><td colspan="4">Controlled opening equipment or automatic piloting systems designed for parachuted loads;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>j.</td><td colspan="4">“Sub-orbital craft” and related equipment, as follows, and specially designed or modified components therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3">1.</td><td>“Sub-orbital craft”;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3">2.</td><td>Launch equipment, recovery equipment and ground support equipment;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3">3.</td><td>Equipment designed for command or control.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">. . .</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-7e57303baaf901bb8c75f6bfead4bcc1][^key-b6f97aa1ed6266e1303ead8f2bf45eb8][^key-90d84669b1a0bfa9b282dee83a679b30][^key-1c531bf48ad7e801d5a14e8f182f37f5][^key-73370def0bba85561cc49ac7f9032bea][^key-3fd4ca91e3a9a22a485a3f139f9b59d2][^key-8ca50dbef5a2b65e9c8fc2401b320dc2][^key-a0c23699a9ece073e9c347d4a0f3fd2d][^key-e052629385ccd58990099137202b6eb7][^key-46f69248298d3d10533cd55ce6fdae0a][^key-e6deb052382c79915b70743c173cbdb3][^key-36053b4d2f1d1e91f0dd1d1d582471c5][^key-fdab5afb900efb180ea4673b33f86230][^key-7a0dbfeb91d93a6f07be059f1abca4e5][^key-3359df58fb674dddf25592b2428ce041]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML11</strong></td><td colspan="3">Electronic equipment, “spacecraft” and components, not specified elsewhere in this Schedule, as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="2">Electronic equipment specially designed or modified for military use and specially designed components therefor;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>ML11.a. controls all electronic guidance and navigation equipment.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="2">Jamming equipment designed or modified to hinder the reception, operation or effectiveness of positioning, navigation or timing services provided by “satellite navigation systems”, and specially designed components therefor;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="2">“Spacecraft” specially designed or modified for military use, and “spacecraft” components specially designed for military use.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-b2ce9d624887552ae121ac4552285a16][^key-b519569049e8102143bacdb797bcbcc7]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML12</strong></td><td colspan="3">High velocity kinetic energy weapon systems and related equipment, as follows, and specially designed components therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="2">Kinetic energy weapon systems specially designed for destruction or effecting mission abort of a target;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td><em>For weapon systems using sub-calibre ammunition or employing solely chemical propulsion, and ammunition therefor, see ML1 to ML4.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="2">Specially designed test and evaluation facilities and test models (e.g. diagnostic instrumentation and targets), for dynamic testing of kinetic energy projectiles and systems.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML13</strong></td><td colspan="4">Armoured or protective goods, constructions, components and accessories, as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="3">Metallic or non-metallic armoured plate, having any of the following characteristics:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="2">Manufactured to comply with a military standard or specification; or</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="2">Suitable for military use;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>For body armour plates, see ML13.d.2..</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="3">Constructions of metallic or non-metallic materials, or combinations thereof, specially designed to provide ballistic protection for military systems and specially designed components therefor;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="3">Helmets and specially designed components and accessories therefor, as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="2">Helmets manufactured according to military standards or specifications, or comparable national standards;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="2">Shells, liners, or comfort pads, specially designed for helmets specified in ML13.c.1.;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="2">Add-on ballistic protection elements, specially designed for helmets specified in ML13.c.1.;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note 1:</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>ML13.c. does not control helmets that meet all of the following:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>a.</em></td><td><em>Were first manufactured before 1970;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>b.</em></td><td><em>Are neither designed or modified to accept, nor equipped with items specified in this Schedule.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note 2:</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>ML13.c. does not control individual helmets when accompanying their users.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>Military high altitude parachutists’ protective headgear is controlled in ML10.h.3..</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>d.</td><td colspan="3">Body armour or protective garments, and components therefor, as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="2">Soft body armour or protective garments, manufactured to military standards or specifications, or to their equivalents, and specially designed components therefor;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note 1:</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>For the purposes of ML13.d.1., military standards or specifications include, at a minimum, specifications for fragmentation protection.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note 2:</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>ML13.d.1. does not control protective eyewear.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>For “laser” protective eyewear, see ML17.o..</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="2">Hard body armour plates providing ballistic protection equal to or better than level III (NIJ 0101.06 July 2008) or equivalent standards.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>ML13.d. does not control individual suits of body armour or ballistic protective garments for personal protection and accessories therefor when accompanying their users.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>See also 1A of Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-440193a4fb5fc2ceb5a818317bf51510][^key-02c5ece7ff680dc08cc680e8371d7446][^key-45169bb4bbd1c2e543c9340c8946f581][^key-8e7f94e11d37f87d69a8caffb16435ac][^key-fabe832e0f5ec4da0f90e04c1e2046d9][^key-7617126fb25924b488a38bf116aa2357][^key-50c3433256c5a2b1d8eb6de2a7599e2c]

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| **ML14** | Specialised equipment for military training or for simulating military scenarios, simulators specially designed for training in the use of any firearm or weapon specified in ML1 or ML2, and specially designed components and accessories therefor. |

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML15</strong></td><td colspan="3">Imaging or countermeasure equipment, as follows, specially designed for military use, and specially designed components and accessories therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="2">Recorders and image processing equipment;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="2">Cameras, photographic equipment and film processing equipment;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="2">Image intensifier equipment;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>d.</td><td colspan="2">Infrared or thermal imaging equipment;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>e.</td><td colspan="2">Imaging radar sensor equipment;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>f.</td><td colspan="2">Countermeasure or counter-countermeasure equipment for the equipment specified in ML15.a. to ML15.e.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td>ML15.f. includes equipment designed to degrade the operation or effectiveness of military imaging systems or to minimise such degrading effects.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td><em>ML15 does not control “first generation image intensifier tubes” or equipment specially designed so that only “first generation image intensifier tubes” are or can be incorporated in it.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B. 1:</em></td><td><em>For weapons sights incorporating “first generation image intensifier tubes” see ML1., ML2. and ML5.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B. 2:</em></td><td><em>See also 6A of Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-df2fd7a02a9ecab9634f8b4da3ccce8f]

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| **ML16** | Forgings, castings and other unfinished goods, specially designed for any of the goods specified in ML1 to ML4, ML6, ML9, ML10, ML12 or ML19. |

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML17</strong></td><td colspan="4">Miscellaneous goods, material and “libraries”, as follows, and specially designed components therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="3">Diving and underwater swimming apparatus, specially designed or modified for military use, as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">1.</td><td>Self-contained diving rebreathers, closed or semi-closed circuit;</td></tr>
<tr><td rowspan="2"></td><td rowspan="2"></td><td colspan="2">2.</td><td>Underwater swimming apparatus specially designed for use with the diving apparatus specified in ML17.a.1.;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2"><em>N.B.:</em></td><td><em>See also 8A002.q. of Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="3">Construction equipment specially designed for military use;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="3">Fittings, coatings and treatments for signature suppression, specially designed for military use;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>d.</td><td colspan="3">Field engineer equipment specially designed for use in a combat zone;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>e.</td><td colspan="3">“Robots”, “robot” controllers and “robot” “end-effectors”, meeting any of the following descriptions:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">1.</td><td>Specially designed for military use;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">2.</td><td>Incorporating means of protecting hydraulic lines against externally induced punctures caused by ballistic fragments (e.g. incorporating self-sealing lines) and designed to use hydraulic fluids with flash points higher than 839 K (566<sup>o</sup>C); or</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">3.</td><td>Specially designed or rated for operating in an ‘electromagnetic pulse’ (‘EMP’) environment;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"><em>Technical Note:</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>For the purposes of ML17.e.3., ‘EMP’ does not refer to unintentional interference caused by electromagnetic radiation from nearby equipment (e.g. machinery, appliances or electronics) or lightning.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>f.</td><td colspan="3">“Libraries” specially designed or modified for military use with systems, equipment or components, specified in this Schedule;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>g.</td><td colspan="3">Nuclear power generating equipment or propulsion equipment, not specified elsewhere in this Schedule, specially designed for military use and components therefor, specially designed or modified for military use;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"><em>Note:</em></td><td><em>ML17.g. includes “nuclear reactors”</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>h.</td><td colspan="3">Goods and material, coated, treated or prepared to provide signature suppression, specially designed for military use, not controlled elsewhere in this Schedule;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>i.</td><td colspan="3">Simulators specially designed for military “nuclear reactors”;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>j.</td><td colspan="3">Mobile repair shops specially designed or modified to service military equipment;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>k.</td><td colspan="3">Field generators specially designed or modified for military use;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>l.</td><td colspan="3">ISO intermodal containers or demountable vehicle bodies (i.e., swap bodies), specially designed or modified for military use;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>m.</td><td colspan="3">Ferries, not controlled elsewhere in this Schedule, ... bridges and pontoons, specially designed for military use;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>n.</td><td colspan="3">Test models specially designed for the “development” of goods specified in ML4, ML6, ML9 or ML10;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>o.</td><td colspan="3">“Laser” protection equipment (e.g. eye or sensor protection) specially designed for military use;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>p.</td><td colspan="3">“Fuel cells”, other than those which form components of items controlled elsewhere in this Schedule, specially designed or modified for military use.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-3d21fbb8ee79e6e7a58f7b12de470aaa][^key-af951f9edcac25e4fa06263f6525d350][^key-e4e25c7b94d65ac3e7dc63b1cd634028][^key-d876d3d154a1eb651220ce3b2d0c5071][^key-124718a124f2fe0ebc911177cc73694b][^key-efc4eac14a3b903d7bde802eea9b75c1][^key-7c67be21fd172dfe4210caaa12e0f052][^key-f3e4e3dc18a468b7b0a952d8a7e2ead3][^key-29439fd4bc3051bbafa96d6215bb07e0][^key-14e1b40743bcbff535eb9af963497738]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML18</strong></td><td colspan="2">“Production” equipment , environmental test facilities, and components, as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td>Equipment specially designed or modified ... for the “production” of goods specified in this Schedule, and specially designed components therefor;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td>Environmental test facilities, specially designed for the certification, qualification or testing of goods specified in this Schedule, and specially designed equipment therefor, not specified elsewhere in this Schedule</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-cb676510973abdb1cbd1ff3c267b3517][^key-7e253c518634c793d4d7b9ed5713a5f2][^key-5e3c16c821a2921a805bb215d3c3e141][^key-e437fddefb7f17482d0ae0936b6a2eac]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML19</strong></td><td colspan="2">Directed energy weapon (DEW) systems, related or countermeasure equipment and test models, as follows, and specially designed components therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td>“Laser” ‘weapon systems’ not specified by ML19.f.;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td>Particle beam ‘weapon systems’;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>c.</td><td>High power Radio-Frequency (RF) ‘weapon systems’;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>d.</td><td>Equipment specially designed for the detection or identification of, or defence against, systems specified in ML19.a. to ML19.c.;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>e.</td><td>Physical test models for the systems, equipment and components specified in ML19;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>f.</td><td>“Laser” systems specially designed to cause permanent blindness to un-enhanced vision (i.e. to the naked eye or to the eye with corrective eyesight devices).</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2">Technical note:</td><td>For the purposes of ML19, ‘weapon systems’ are designed to damage, destroy or effect mission abort of a target.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-40444c0c13c71cc174e86d7eb4b5e39b][^key-d827688e31020d7f4865bc2b9e8056a1]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML20</strong></td><td colspan="2">Cryogenic and “superconductive” equipment, as follows, and specially designed components and accessories therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td>Equipment specially designed or configured to be installed in a vehicle for military ground, marine, airborne or space applications, capable of operating while in motion and of producing or maintaining temperatures below 103 K (-170<sup>o</sup>C);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td>“Superconductive” electrical equipment (rotating machinery or transformers) specially designed or configured to be installed in a vehicle for military ground, marine, airborne or space applications and capable of operating while in motion.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td><em>ML20 does not control direct-current hybrid homopolar generators that have single-pole normal metal armatures which rotate in a magnetic field produced by superconducting windings, provided those windings are the only superconducting component in the generator.</em></td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-c559a35d9c38fb2e87cac15ccca85c75]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML21</strong></td><td colspan="5">“Software” as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2">a.</td><td colspan="3">“Software” specially designed or modified for any of the following:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="2">“Development”, “production” operation or maintenance of equipment specified in this Schedule;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="2">“Development” or “production” of materials specified in this Schedule; or</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="2">“Development”, “production”, operation or maintenance of “software” specified in this Schedule.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2">b.</td><td colspan="3">Specific “software”, other than that specified in ML21.a., as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2">1.</td><td>“Software” that is both specially designed for military use and specially designed for modelling, simulating or evaluating military weapons systems;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2">2.</td><td>“Software” that is both specially designed for military use and specially designed for modelling or simulating military operational scenarios;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2">3.</td><td>“Software” for determining the effects of conventional, nuclear, chemical or biological weapons;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2">4.</td><td>“Software” that is both specially designed for military use and specially designed for Command, Communications, Control and Intelligence (C<sup>3</sup>I) applications or Command, Communications, Control, Computer and Intelligence (C<sup>4</sup>I) applications;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2">5.</td><td>“Software” specially designed or modified for the conduct of military offensive cyber operations;<br><em>Note 1: ML21.b.5. includes “software” designed to destroy, damage, degrade or disrupt systems, equipment or “software”, specified in this Schedule, and associated cyber reconnaissance and cyber command and control “software”.</em><br><em>Note 2: ML21.b.5. does not apply to a “vulnerability disclosure” or to a “cyber incident response”, limited to non-military defensive cybersecurity readiness or response.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2">c.</td><td colspan="3">“Software”, not specified in ML21.a. or ML21.b., specially designed or modified to enable equipment not specified in this Schedule to perform the military functions of equipment specified in this Schedule;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2">N.B.:</td><td>For general purpose “digital computers” with installed “software” specified by ML21.c., see systems, equipment or components specified in this schedule.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2">d.</td><td colspan="3">Other “software” specially designed or modified for military use.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>N.B.:</em></td><td><em>Source code for “software” is controlled in ML22.</em></td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-03e40e2f8088d3bfa441cae67ae4d5e6][^key-d248cec453262e239745640cea8cc720]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>PL5001</strong></td><td colspan="6">Other security and para-military police goods as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="5">Acoustic devices represented by the manufacturers or suppliers thereof as suitable for riot control purposes, and specially designed components therefor;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="5">Anti-riot and ballistic shields and specially designed components therefor;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="3"><em>N.B.:</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>See also 2.1 of Annex III to “the torture Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="5">Shackles designed for restraining human beings having an overall dimension including chain, when measured from the outer edge of one cuff to the outer edge of the other cuff, of between 240mm and 280mm when locked;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4"><em>N.B.:</em></td><td><em>See also 1.1 of Annex III to “the torture Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>d.</td><td colspan="5">Water cannon and specially designed components therefor;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2"><em>N.B.:</em></td><td colspan="3"><em>See also 3.6 of Annex III to “the torture Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>e.</td><td colspan="5">Riot control vehicles which have been specially designed or modified to be electrified to repel boarders and components therefor specially designed or modified for that purpose;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>f.</td><td colspan="5">Components specially designed or modified for portable devices designed or modified for the purposes of riot control or self-protection by the administration of an electric shock (e.g. electric-shock batons, electric-shock shields, stun-guns and electric-shock dart-guns).</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2"><em>N.B.:</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>See also article 42S of this Order.</em></td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-f3d9b8a4ff978581f859d38b1faedc16]

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>ML22</strong></td><td colspan="3">“Technology” as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="2">“Technology”, other than “technology” specified in ML22.b., which is “required” for the “development”, “production”, operation, installation, maintenance (checking), repair, overhaul or refurbishing of goods or “software” specified in this Schedule;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="2">“Technology” as follows:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td>“Technology” “required” for the design of, the assembly of components into, and the operation, maintenance and repair of complete production installations for goods specified in this Schedule, even if the components of such production installations are not specified;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td>This entry is not used;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td>This entry is not used;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>4.</td><td>“Technology” “required” exclusively for the incorporation of “biocatalysts”, specified in ML7.i.1, into military carrier substances or military material.</td></tr>

## SCHEDULE 3 — UK CONTROLLED DUAL-USE GOODS, SOFTWARE AND TECHNOLOGY[^key-4dbe5ed00b57f90de421d29075d6b425]

Articles 2 and 4

*Note: In this Schedule, defined terms are printed in quotation marks.*

Definitions

In this Schedule:

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="3">“ammunition” means the complete round or the components thereof, including cartridge cases, primers, propellant powder, bullets or projectiles that are used in a “firearm”;<br>...<br>“development” means all stages prior to “production” (eg, design, design research, design analyses, design concepts, assembly and testing of prototypes, pilot production schemes, design data, process of transforming design data into goods or “software”, configuration design, integration design, layouts);<br>“Deactivation Regulation” means Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) 2015/2403 establishing common guidelines on deactivation standards and techniques for ensuring that deactivated firearms are rendered irreversibly inoperable.<br>...<br>...<br>...<br>“energetic materials” means substances or mixtures that react chemically to release energy required for their intended application; “explosives”, “pyrotechnics” and “propellants” are sub-classes of energetic materials;<br>“essential components” means the breech-closing mechanism, the chamber and the barrel of a “firearm”, which, being separate objects, are included in the category of the “firearms” on which they are or are intended to be mounted;<br>“explosive signatures” are features which are characteristic of explosives in any form prior to their initiation, as detected using technology including, but not limited to, ion mobility spectrometry, chemiluminescence, fluorescence, nuclear, acoustic or electromagnetic techniques;<br>“explosives” means solid, liquid or gaseous substances or mixtures of substances which, in their application as primary, booster, or main charges in warheads, demolition and other applications, are required to detonate;<br>“firearm” means any portable barrelled weapon that expels, is designed to expel or may be converted to expel, a shot, bullet or projectile by the action of a combustible propellant;<br><em>Note: This definition does not include items specially designed for any of the following:</em><br><em>a. Alarm;</em><br><em>b. Signalling;</em><br><em>c. Life-saving;</em><br><em>d. Animal slaughter;</em><br><em>e. Harpoon fishing;</em><br><em>f. Industrial or technical purposes.</em><br>“<strong>Firearm Regulation</strong>”, in relation to—<br>England and Wales and Scotland, means Regulation (EU) No 258/2012 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 14 March 2012 implementing Article 10 of the United Nations' Protocol against the illicit manufacturing of and trafficking in firearms, their parts and components and ammunition, supplementing the United Nations Convention against Transnational Organised Crime (UN Firearms Protocol), and establishing export authorisation, and import and transit measures for firearms, their parts and components and ammunition;<br>Northern Ireland, means Regulation (EU) No 258/2012 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 14 March 2012 implementing Article 10 of the United Nations' Protocol against the illicit manufacturing of and trafficking in firearms, their parts and components and ammunition, supplementing the United Nations Convention against Transnational Organised Crime (UN Firearms Protocol), and establishing export authorisation, and import and transit measures for firearms, their parts and components and ammunition as it has effect by virtue of the Protocol on Ireland/Northern Ireland in the EU withdrawal agreement;<br>“improvised explosive devices” means devices fabricated or intended to be placed in an improvised manner incorporating destructive, lethal, noxious, “pyrotechnic” or incendiary chemicals designed to destroy, disfigure or harass; they may incorporate military stores, but are normally devised from non-military components;<br>“lighter-than-air vehicles” means balloons and airships that rely on hot air or on lighter-than-air gases such as helium or hydrogen for their lift;<br>“parts” means any element or replacement element as referred to specifically designed for a “firearm” and essential to its operation, including a barrel, frame or receiver, slide or cylinder, bolt or breech block ...<br>“previously separated” in relation to a controlled isotope in any form, means after the application of any process intended to increase the concentration of the controlled isotope;<br>...<br>“production” means all production stages (eg, product engineering, manufacture, integration, assembly (mounting), inspection, testing, quality assurance);<br>“propellants” means substances or mixtures that react chemically to produce large volumes of hot gases at controlled rates to perform mechanical work;<br>“pyrotechnic(s)” means mixtures of solid or liquid fuels and oxidisers which, when ignited, undergo an energetic chemical reaction at a controlled rate intended to produce specific time delays, or quantities of heat, noise, smoke, visible light or infrared radiation; pyrophorics are a subclass of pyrotechnics, which contain no oxidisers but ignite spontaneously on contact with air;<br>“required” as applied to “technology”, refers to only that portion of “technology” which is peculiarly responsible for achieving or exceeding the controlled performance levels, characteristics or functions. Such “required” “technology” may be shared by different goods ...;<br>“technology” means specific ‘information’ necessary for the “development”, “production” or “use” of goods or “software”;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2"><em>Technical Note:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2"><em>‘Information’ may take forms including, not limited to: blueprints, plans, diagrams, models, formulae, tables, ‘source code’, engineering designs and specifications, manuals and instructions written or recorded on other media or devices (eg, disk, tape, read-only memories);</em></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="2"><em>‘source code’ (or source language) is a convenient expression of one or more processes which may be turned by a programming system into equipment executable form.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="3">“Unmanned Aerial Vehicle” (or “UAV”) means any “aircraft” capable of initiating flight and sustaining controlled flight and navigation without any human presence on board;<br>“use” means operation, installation (eg, on-site installation), maintenance, checking, repair, overhaul and refurbishing;<br>“vaccines” are medical products in a pharmaceutical formulation licensed by, or having marketing or clinical trial authorisation from, the regulatory authorities of either the country of manufacture or of use, which is intended to stimulate a protective immunological response in humans or animals in order to prevent disease in those to whom or to which it is administered.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[^key-98424d60412226323f6a2dd2b06b3d6b][^key-103dc7e58308c119463ba9f45dceb495][^key-9bf4b2669f5063f9a2b93bca1e823e58][^key-c49f15ab2d5ddb3decf4d8d5a104400d][^key-4cfb93c3ab215ff437d84531e7471540][^key-adcaec5f50cff1c360d1717ea66488f7][^key-9e47b91218c57a47490d10dec6654894][^key-509caa8b3e604709246c8bdea5a46c69][^key-85a5895e8da8f46d4f7b70963f494b28][^key-4cd24484ce7846345892515333ce6551][^key-6a2ed6fa3469d385920e55623eb9abb9][^key-919de1d168793e5175d14f285011f061][^key-86461adf34423f017127e47c5f898fbb][^key-9af85eb1c798e28427ae4423534f76fb][^key-ed40241b729be99f2496cf348c889559][^key-0f21eb2a906f3a8fbfd620e0a93d5ee8][^key-e06adc0df14ff60baf72a62f3af69381]

Explosive-related goods and technology

<table>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>PL8001</strong></td><td colspan="4">The export or “transfer by electronic means” of the following goods or “technology” is prohibited to any destination outside all of the following: European Union, Australia, New Zealand, Canada, Norway, Switzerland, United States of America and Japan:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td colspan="4"></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>a.</td><td colspan="3">Equipment and devices, other than those in Schedule 2 or in 1A004.d., 1A005, 1A006, 1A007, 1A008, 3A229, 3A232 or 5A001.h. in Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”, for detection of or use with “explosives” or for dealing with or protecting against “improvised explosive devices”, as follows, and specially designed components therefor:</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>1.</td><td colspan="2">Electronic equipment designed to detect “explosives” or “explosive signatures”;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td><em>See also 1A004.d. in Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td><em>PL8001.a.1. does not control equipment requiring operator judgment to establish the presence of “explosives” or “explosive signatures”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>2.</td><td colspan="2">Electronic jamming equipment specially designed to prevent the detonation by radio remote control of “improvised explosive devices”;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td><em>See also 5A001.h. in Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>3.</td><td colspan="2">Equipment and devices specially designed to initiate explosions by electrical or non-electrical means, (eg, firing sets, detonators and igniters);</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td><em>See also 1A007, 1A008, 3A229 and 3A232 in Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td><em>PL8001.a.3. does not control:</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>a.</em></td><td><em>Equipment and devices specially designed for a specific commercial use consisting of the actuation or operation by explosive means of other equipment or devices the function of which is not the initiation or creation of explosions;</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>b.</em></td><td><em>Pressure controlled equipment specially designed for down-hole oilfield equipment applications and which are incapable of use at atmospheric pressure; ...</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>c.</em></td><td><em>Detonating cord; and</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>d.</em></td><td><em>Equipment and devices specially designed for use with pyrotechnics.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td>4.</td><td colspan="2">Equipment and devices, including, but not limited to: shields and helmets, specially designed for the disposal of “improvised explosive devices”;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B.:</em></td><td><em>See also 1A005, 1A006 and 5A001.h. in Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td><em>Note:</em></td><td><em>PL8001.a.4. does not control bomb blankets, mechanical handling equipment for manoeuvring or exposing “improvised explosive devices”, containers designed for holding “improvised explosive devices” or objects suspected of being such devices or other equipment specially designed to temporarily protect against “improvised explosive devices” or objects suspected of being such devices.</em></td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="3">Linear cutting explosive charges other than those listed at entry 1A008 of Annex I to “the dual-use Regulation”;</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="3">“Technology” “required” for the “use” of goods in PL8001.a. and PL8001.b.”</td></tr>
<tr><td></td><td></td><td><em>N.B.</em></td><td colspan="2"><em>See article 18 of this Order for exceptions from the controls on “technology”.</em></td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

Materials, chemicals, micro-organisms and toxins

<table>
<tbody>

<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td>b.</td><td colspan="5">Nitroglycerol;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td>c.</td><td colspan="5">Pentaerythritol tetranitrate (PETN);</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td>d.</td><td colspan="5">Picryl chloride;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td>e.</td><td colspan="5">Trinitrophenylmethylnitramine (tetryl);</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td>f.</td><td colspan="5">2,4,6-Trinitrotoluene (TNT).</td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="8"></td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">b.</td><td colspan="2">Aero-engines and auxiliary power units (APU)s and specially designed components therefor;</td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">f.</td><td colspan="2">Transmissions and gearboxes, and specially designed components therefor;</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2"></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">g.</td><td colspan="2">“UAV” recovery systems;</td></tr>

Article 45

*(This note is not part of the Order)*

This Order consolidates Orders made under the Export Control Act 2002, with some changes. As a consequence the Export of Goods, Transfer of Technology and Provision of Technical Assistance (Control) Order 2003, the Trade in Goods (Control) Order 2003, the Trade in Controlled Goods (Embargoed Destinations) Order 2004 and the Technical Assistance Control Regulations 2006 and various provisions amending them are revoked (article 45).

This Order is now where penalty and licensing provisions are to be found relating to Council Regulation [(EC) No 1334/2000](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/regulation/2000/1334) (the “**dual-use Regulation**”) and Council Regulation [(EC) No 1236/2005](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/regulation/2005/1236) (the “**torture Regulation**”). These cover, respectively, goods, software and technology that can be used for both civil and military purposes and goods that can be used for capital punishment, torture or other cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment. Part 2 of the Order also supplements the directly applicable provisions of these Regulations by extending their controls, in particular to cover additional goods (see articles 4 and 5), intra-Community transfers and goods in transit (in the circumstances set out in 6, 7, 8 and 9) and different types of transfers of technology (see articles 10, 11 and 12).

Military goods, software and technology are not covered by the Community legislation so these are the subject of article 3. The list of military goods, software and technology in Schedule 2 is based on the Wassenaar Arrangement military list (the Wassenaar Arrangement is an international regime for controlling conventional arms) but national controls (listed as “PL . . .”) have been added.

The remaining provisions of Part 2 are exceptions from the controls, notably covering scheduled flights and other low-risk movements of aircraft and vessels, firearms (to the extent they are the subject of other controls pursuant to Council Directive [91/477/EEC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/1991/0477)), transit and transhipment, and transfers of software and technology that are of little concern. The extent of the transit and transhipment exception varies according to the type of goods and their destination. A change from the previous Orders is that a more extensive list of countries is subject to transit and transhipment controls in relation to a range of sensitive items including small arms and light weapons.

Part 3 contains a prohibition relating to the provision of technical assistance in relation to chemical, biological or nuclear weapons programmes.

Part 4 contains controls relating to movements of goods between countries other than the United Kingdom and the Isle of Man. The level of control varies according to the sensitivity of the goods, which are organised into three categories (A to C, A being goods with the highest level of sensitivity such as torture equipment), and category B now includes light weapons. In relation to both category A and B there are controls on United Kingdom persons acting outside the United Kingdom. However, for category B, certain ancillary activities are not covered (see article 22(3)). Transportation services are newly covered for this category but drivers, pilots etc. providing services to transport contractors already within the controls are not caught in their own right. Category C exempts transportation services completely along with other ancillary activities (see article 23(2)).

Parts 5 to 7 all relate back to the earlier provisions. Part 5 contains provisions about licences. In particular, there are provisions about record keeping by licence holders (articles 29 and 30) and inspection of the relevant records (article 31) and appeals from licensing decisions (article 33). Part 6 contains provisions about offences and the powers of Her Majesty's Revenue and Customs who will enforce the Order. Offences relating to exportation out of the United Kingdom without a licence are dealt with in the Customs and Excise Management Act 1979 (sections 68 and 170). Part 7, as well as the revocations noted above, contains provisions about the use of information received in the course of operating the controls (article 43).

An Impact Assessment has been prepared in respect of this Order and is annexed to the Explanatory Memorandum which is available alongside the instrument on the OPSI website.
